        Idea:
Final polish pass on shot creation system. Four items: (1) Remove target_timeline_frame escape hatch from duplicate_as_new_generation RPC. The SQL currently has a branch that skips its own collision logic when target_timeline_frame is passed. Remove this parameter from the RPC signature and the SQL branch, so SQL always computes frame placement. Update the TypeScript types and any callers. (2) Remove debug console.log statements left from the debugging session. Search for [ShotCreate:resolve], [ShotCreate:path], [ShotCreate] in TimelineEditor.tsx and shotCreationPaths.ts. Remove all of them. (3) Investigate and document the Node.contains() portal issue. The three portaled menus (ClipContextMenu in ClipAction.tsx, SelectionContextMenu.tsx, TimelineCanvas shot group menu) all have stopPropagation workarounds because Node.contains() returns false for elements inside the portal. Write a minimal repro or investigate React 18 portal behavior to determine root cause. If fixable, fix it and remove the workarounds. If not fixable, add a clear shared comment block explaining the root cause so future developers understand. (4) Migrate addImageToShot mutation (useShotGenerationMutations.ts) from the old optimistic-then-replace pattern with temp IDs to the same atomic pattern. Currently it creates temp IDs in onMutate, writes to DB in mutationFn, then replaces temp IDs in onSuccess. The DB write (runAddImageMutation -> insertAutoPositionedShotGeneration) already returns the real data. Simplify: remove the optimistic temp ID scaffolding, use the DB response directly to update cache via upsertShotInCache or direct setQueryData, remove replaceOptimisticItemInCache/replaceOptimisticItemInShotsCache.

        Debt watch items (do not make these worse):
- [DEBT] agent-chat-rendering: removing the duplicate assistant turn may hide message_user text if the frontend only renders assistant-role turns as chat bubbles. (flagged 1 times across 1 plans)
- [DEBT] agent-chat-undo-integration: agent chat edits arrive via query invalidation/realtime polling and will bypass the undo stack because those paths are marked skiphistory. (flagged 1 times across 1 plans)
- [DEBT] agent-tool-utils: agent-tool-utils: step 6 moves `isrecord` out of `llm/messages.ts` without preserving that module's current export surface for `index.ts`, `db.ts`, `tool-calls.ts`, and `selectedclips.ts`, so the refactor plan can break four existing imports unless it adds a re-export or rewires those callers. (flagged 2 times across 1 plans)
- [DEBT] are-the-proposed-changes-technically-correct: are the proposed changes technically correct?: checked the actual export surface in `supabase/functions/ai-timeline-agent/llm/messages.ts` and its consumers. step 6 says to 'remove local `isrecord`' from `messages.ts` and import it from `utils.ts`, but without also preserving `messages.ts` as an exporter or rewiring `index.ts`, `db.ts`, `tool-calls.ts`, and `selectedclips.ts`, that change would break the module graph immediately. (flagged 2 times across 1 plans)
- [DEBT] are-the-proposed-changes-technically-correct: are the proposed changes technically correct?: checked the render-gating cleanup plan against remotion's runtime exports in `node_modules/remotion/dist/cjs/cancel-render.js:46-70`. `cancelrender()` stores an error and throws to abort the render; it is not a benign cleanup for an abandoned `delayrender` handle, so step 2's instruction to call `cancelrender(handle)` on unmount is technically wrong and would abort preview or export with an error. (flagged 1 times across 1 plans)
- [DEBT] are-the-proposed-changes-technically-correct: are the proposed changes technically correct?: checked how remotion itself acquires delay handles in `node_modules/remotion/dist/cjs/iframe.js:8-18`. remotion's own components create the handle synchronously with `usestate(() => delayrender(...))`; the plan says 'calls delayrender() on mount', which reads like an effect-time action and leaves the initial-frame blocking behavior underspecified compared with the actual library pattern. (flagged 1 times across 1 plans)
- [DEBT] are-the-proposed-changes-technically-correct: are the proposed changes technically correct?: checked the new `useaudioparam()` sketch against `src/tools/video-editor/effects/compileeffect.tsx:1-2`. the plan defines it in terms of `interpolate(audio[binding.source], ...)` but does not explicitly import `interpolate` into the new hook file or simplify it to straight arithmetic, so the helper's implementation contract is still looser than it needs to be. (flagged 1 times across 1 plans)
- [DEBT] are-the-success-criteria-well-prioritized-and-verifiable: are the success criteria well-prioritized and verifiable?: checked the two user-visible `must` goals for the selection ring and locked-pane chat. they describe the right product outcomes, but they are mostly manual ui behaviors rather than code-review-verifiable gates, so they are not fully testable from the listed automated checks alone. (flagged 2 times across 1 plans)
- [DEBT] are-the-success-criteria-well-prioritized-and-verifiable: are the success criteria well-prioritized and verifiable?: checked the revised `must` criterion about eliminating duplicated server-side helpers. as written, the plan could satisfy 'all import from utils.ts' for `generation.ts`, `create-task.ts`, and `messages.ts` and still break four existing `messages.ts` consumers, so the criterion is incomplete unless it also requires preserving or updating the `messages.ts` export surface. (flagged 2 times across 1 plans)
- [DEBT] are-the-success-criteria-well-prioritized-and-verifiable: are the success criteria well-prioritized and verifiable?: checked the `must` criterion 'edge function tests (ai-generate-effect/index.test.ts) pass with updated templates' against the actual test file. since `supabase/functions/ai-generate-effect/index.test.ts` mocks `buildgenerateeffectmessages()`, this criterion can pass without validating the template edits themselves, so it is still a weak `must` gate as written. v_source_params\n+  FROM public.generations g\n+  LEFT JOIN public.generation_variants gv ON gv.id = g.primary_variant_id\n+  WHERE g.id = p_generation_id\n+    AND g.project_id = p_project_id;\n+\n+ 'timeline_duplicate',\n+      'source_generation_id', p_generation_id,\n+      'duplicated_at', statement_timestamp()\n+    );\n+\n+  INSERT INTO public.generations (\n+    project_id,\n+    type,\n+ v_created_at;\n+\n+  INSERT INTO public.generation_variants (\n+    generation_id,\n+    project_id,\n+    location,\n+    thumbnail_url,\n+    is_primary,\n+    variant_type,\n+    name,\n+    params\n+  )\n+  VALUES (\n+    v_new_generation_id,\n+    p_project_id,\n+    v_source_location,\n+    v_source_thumbnail_url,\n+    TRUE,\n+    'original',\n+    'Original',\n+    v_new_params\n+  );\n+\n+  v_new_timeline_frame := CASE\n+    WHEN p_next_timeline_frame IS NOT NULL THEN floor((p_timeline_frame + p_next_timeline_frame) / 2.0)::integer\n+    ELSE p_timeline_frame + 30\n+  END;\n+\n+  v_new_timeline_frame := GREATEST(0, round(v_new_timeline_frame::numeric)::integer);\n+\n+  IF EXISTS (\n+    SELECT 1\n+    FROM public.shot_generations sg\n+    WHERE sg.shot_id =    END IF;\n+\n+      IF v_new_timeline_frame - v_offset >= 0 AND NOT EXISTS (\n+        SELECT 1\n+        FROM public.shot_generations sg\n+        WHERE sg.shot_id = p_shot_id\n+          AND sg.timeline_frame = v_new_timeline_frame - v_offset\n+      ) THEN\n+        v_new_timeline_frame := v_new_timeline_frame - v_offset;\n+        EXIT;\n+      END IF;\n+\n+      v_offset := v_offset + 1;\n+    END LOOP;\n+  END IF;\n+\n+  INSERT INTO public.shot_generations (\n+    shot_id,\n+    generation_id,\n+    timeline_frame\n+  )\n+  VALUES (\n+    p_shot_id,\n+    v_new_generation_id,\n+         Idea:
Final polish pass on shot creation system. Four items: (1) Remove target_timeline_frame escape hatch from duplicate_as_new_generation RPC. The SQL currently has a branch that skips its own collision logic when target_timeline_frame is passed. Remove this parameter from the RPC signature and the SQL branch, so SQL always computes frame placement. Update the TypeScript types and any callers. (2) Remove debug console.log statements left from the debugging session. Search for [ShotCreate:resolve], [ShotCreate:path], [ShotCreate] in TimelineEditor.tsx and shotCreationPaths.ts. Remove all of them. (3) Investigate and document the Node.contains() portal issue. The three portaled menus (ClipContextMenu in ClipAction.tsx, SelectionContextMenu.tsx, TimelineCanvas shot group menu) all have stopPropagation workarounds because Node.contains() returns false for elements inside the portal. Write a minimal repro or investigate React 18 portal behavior to determine root cause. If fixable, fix it and remove the workarounds. If not fixable, add a clear shared comment block explaining the root cause so future developers understand. (4) Migrate addImageToShot mutation (useShotGenerationMutations.ts) from the old optimistic-then-replace pattern with temp IDs to the same atomic pattern. Currently it creates temp IDs in onMutate, writes to DB in mutationFn, then replaces temp IDs in onSuccess. The DB write (runAddImageMutation -> insertAutoPositionedShotGeneration) already returns the real data. Simplify: remove the optimistic temp ID scaffolding, use the DB response directly to update cache via upsertShotInCache or direct setQueryData, remove replaceOptimisticItemInCache/replaceOptimisticItemInShotsCache.

The SQL currently has a branch that skips its own collision logic when target_timeline_frame is passed. Remove this parameter from the RPC signature and the SQL branch, so SQL always computes frame placement. Update the TypeScript types and any callers. (2) Remove debug console.log statements left from the debugging session. Search for [ShotCreate:resolve], [ShotCreate:path], [ShotCreate] in TimelineEditor.tsx and shotCreationPaths.ts. Remove all of them. (3) Investigate and document the Node.contains() portal issue. The three portaled menus (ClipContextMenu in ClipAction.tsx, SelectionContextMenu.tsx, TimelineCanvas shot group menu) all have stopPropagation workarounds because Node.contains() returns false for elements inside the portal. Write a minimal repro or investigate React 18 portal behavior to determine root cause. If fixable, fix it and remove the workarounds. If not fixable, add a clear shared comment block explaining the root cause so future developers understand. (4) Migrate addImageToShot mutation (useShotGenerationMutations.ts) from the old optimistic-then-replace pattern with temp IDs to the same atomic pattern. Currently it creates temp IDs in onMutate, writes to DB in mutationFn, then replaces temp IDs in onSuccess. The DB write (runAddImageMutation -> insertAutoPositionedShotGeneration) already returns the real data. Simplify: remove the optimistic temp ID scaffolding, use the DB response directly to update cache via upsertShotInCache or direct setQueryData, remove replaceOptimisticItemInCache/replaceOptimisticItemInShotsCache.

        Debt watch items (do not make these worse):
- [DEBT] agent-chat-rendering: removing the duplicate assistant turn may hide message_user text if the frontend only renders assistant-role turns as chat bubbles. (flagged 1 times across 1 plans)
- [DEBT] agent-chat-undo-integration: agent chat edits arrive via query invalidation/realtime polling and will bypass the undo stack because those paths are marked skiphistory. (flagged 1 times across 1 plans)

(flagged 2 times across 1 plans)
- [DEBT] are-the-proposed-changes-technically-correct: are the proposed changes technically correct?: checked the actual export surface in `supabase/functions/ai-timeline-agent/llm/messages.ts` and its consumers. step 6 says to 'remove local `isrecord`' from `messages.ts` and import it from `utils.ts`, but without also preserving `messages.ts` as an exporter or rewiring `index.ts`, `db.ts`, `tool-calls.ts`, and `selectedclips.ts`, that change would break the module graph immediately. (flagged 2 times across 1 plans)

(flagged 1 times across 1 plans)
- [DEBT] are-the-proposed-changes-technically-correct: are the proposed changes technically correct?: checked how remotion itself acquires delay handles in `node_modules/remotion/dist/cjs/iframe.js:8-18`. remotion's own components create the handle synchronously with `usestate(() => delayrender(...))`; the plan says 'calls delayrender() on mount', which reads like an effect-time action and leaves the initial-frame blocking behavior underspecified compared with the actual library pattern. (flagged 1 times across 1 plans)
